					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Kyrgyzstan</span></h1>
							
<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang"  >
	<input type="checkbox" id="p-lang-btn-checkbox" role="button" aria-haspopup="true" data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector" aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 242 languages"   >
	<label id="p-lang-btn-label" for="p-lang-btn-checkbox" class="vector-dropdown-label cdx-button cdx-button--fake-button cdx-button--fake-button--enabled cdx-button--weight-quiet cdx-button--action-progressive mw-portlet-lang-heading-242" aria-hidden="true"  ><span class="vector-icon mw-ui-icon-language-progressive mw-ui-icon-wikimedia-language-progressive"></span>

<span class="vector-dropdown-label-text">242 languages</span>
	</label>
	<div class="vector-dropdown-content">

		<div class="vector-menu-content">
			
			<ul class="vector-menu-content-list">
				
				<li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-ace mw-list-item"><a href="https://ace.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrgyzstan" title="Kyrgyzstan – Achinese" lang="ace" hreflang="ace" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Acèh</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-kbd mw-list-item"><a href="https://kbd.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9A%D1%8A%D1%8B%D1%80%D0%B3%D1%8A%D1%8B%D0%B7%D1%81%D1%82%D1%8D%D0%BD" title="Къыргъызстэн – Kabardian" lang="kbd" hreflang="kbd" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Адыгэбзэ</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-ady mw-list-item"><a <figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bischkek.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Bischkek.jpg/310px-Bischkek.jpg" decoding="async" width="310" height="177" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Bischkek.jpg/465px-Bischkek.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/df/Bischkek.jpg 2x" data-file-width="483" data-file-height="275" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg/310px-Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg" decoding="async" width="310" height="232" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg/465px-Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg/620px-Kyrgyzstan_Mounts_Kyrgyz_Ala_Too_001.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="479" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyzstan mounts <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_Ala-Too_Range" title="Kyrgyz Ala-Too Range">Kyrgyz Ala-Too</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><b>Kyrgyzstan</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Kyrgyz Republic</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in <a href="/wiki/Central_Asia" title="Central Asia">Central Asia</a>, lying in the <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan" title="Tian Shan">Tian Shan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pamir_Mountains" title="Pamir Mountains">Pamir</a> mountain ranges. <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="List of cities in Kyrgyzstan">largest city</a> of the country. Kyrgyzstan is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a> to <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan-Kyrgyzstan_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Kazakhstan-Kyrgyzstan border">the north</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a> to <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan-Uzbekistan_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyzstan-Uzbekistan border">the west</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a> to <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan-Tajikistan_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyzstan-Tajikistan border">the south</a>, and <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a> to <a href="/wiki/China-Kyrgyzstan_border" class="mw-redirect" title="China-Kyrgyzstan border">the east and southeast</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Ethnic <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">Kyrgyz</a> make up the majority of the country's seven million people, followed by significant minorities of <a href="/wiki/Uzbeks" title="Uzbeks">Uzbeks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Russians" title="Russians">Russians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-stat.kg_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stat.kg-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kyrgyzstan's history spans a variety of cultures and empires. Although geographically isolated by its highly mountainous terrain, Kyrgyzstan has been at the crossroads of several great civilizations as part of the <a href="/wiki/Silk_Road" title="Silk Road">Silk Road</a> along with other commercial routes. Inhabited by a succession of tribes and clans, Kyrgyzstan has periodically fallen under larger domination, for example the <a href="/wiki/Turkic_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Turkic people">Turkic</a> <a href="/wiki/Nomads" class="mw-redirect" title="Nomads">nomads</a>, who trace their ancestry to many Turkic states. It was first established as the <a href="/wiki/Yenisei_Kyrgyz_Khaganate" title="Yenisei Kyrgyz Khaganate">Yenisei Kyrgyz Khaganate</a>. Later, in the 13th century, Kyrgyzstan was conquered by the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongols</a>; it regained independence, but was later invaded by <a href="/wiki/Dzungar_Khanate" title="Dzungar Khanate">Dzungar Khanate</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Dzungar_genocide" title="Dzungar genocide">fall of Dzhungars</a>, Kyrgyz and Kipchaks were an integral part of <a href="/wiki/Kokand_Khanate" class="mw-redirect" title="Kokand Khanate">Kokand Khanate</a>. In 1876, Kyrgyzstan became part of the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a>, and in 1936, the <a href="/wiki/Kirghiz_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Kirghiz Soviet Socialist Republic">Kirghiz Soviet Socialist Republic</a> was formed to become a constituent republic of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>. Following <a href="/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev" title="Mikhail Gorbachev">Mikhail Gorbachev</a>'s democratic reforms in the USSR, in 1990 pro-independence candidate <a href="/wiki/Askar_Akayev" title="Askar Akayev">Askar Akayev</a> was elected president. On 31 August 1991, Kyrgyzstan declared independence from Moscow and a democratic government was established. Kyrgyzstan attained sovereignty as a nation state after the <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissolution of the Soviet Union">breakup of the Soviet Union</a> in 1991.
</p><p>After independence, Kyrgyzstan was officially a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a> <a href="/wiki/Presidential_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Presidential republic">presidential republic</a>; after the <a href="/wiki/Tulip_Revolution" title="Tulip Revolution">Tulip Revolution</a> it became a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_parliamentary_republic" title="Unitary parliamentary republic">unitary parliamentary republic</a>, although it gradually developed an executive president and was governed as a <a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_republic" title="Semi-presidential republic">semi-presidential republic</a> before reverting to a presidential system in 2021. Throughout its existence, the country has continued to endure ethnic conflicts,<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> revolts,<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> economic troubles,<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> transitional governments<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> and political conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kyrgyzstan is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_Economic_Union" title="Eurasian Economic Union">Eurasian Economic Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Collective_Security_Treaty_Organization" title="Collective Security Treaty Organization">Collective Security Treaty Organization</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Shanghai_Cooperation_Organisation" title="Shanghai Cooperation Organisation">Shanghai Cooperation Organisation</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation" title="Organisation of Islamic Cooperation">Organisation of Islamic Cooperation</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_of_Turkic_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Organisation of Turkic States">Organisation of Turkic States</a>, the <a href="/wiki/T%C3%BCrksoy" class="mw-redirect" title="Türksoy">Türksoy</a> community and the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>. It is a <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a> ranked 118th in the <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a>, and is the second poorest country in Central Asia after neighbouring Tajikistan. The country's <a href="/wiki/Transition_economy" title="Transition economy">transitional economy</a> is heavily dependent on deposits of <a href="/wiki/Gold" title="Gold">gold</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coal" title="Coal">coal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uranium" title="Uranium">uranium</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><i>Kyrgyz</i> is derived from the Turkic word for "We are forty", believed to refer to the forty clans of <a href="/wiki/Epic_of_Manas" title="Epic of Manas">Manas</a>, a legendary hero who united forty regional clans. <i><a href="/wiki/-Stan" class="mw-redirect" title="-Stan">-Stan</a></i> is a suffix in <a href="/wiki/Persian_language" title="Persian language">Persian</a> meaning "place of".
</p><p>The 40-ray sun on the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Flag of Kyrgyzstan">flag of Kyrgyzstan</a> is a reference to those same forty tribes and the graphical element in the sun's center depicts the wooden crown, called tunduk, of a <a href="/wiki/Yurt" title="Yurt">yurt</a>—a portable dwelling traditionally used by nomads in the steppes of Central Asia.
</p><p>The country's official name is <i>Kyrgyz Republic</i>, used in international arenas and foreign relations.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> In the English-speaking world, the spelling <i>Kyrgyzstan</i> is commonly used, while its former name <i>Kirghizia</i><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> is rarely used.<sup id="cite_ref-Kirghizia_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kirghizia-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="History of Kyrgyzstan">History of Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_history">Early history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sary_-_Kamysh,_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg/220px-Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg/330px-Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg/440px-Sary_-_Kamysh%2C_Kyrgyz_Republic_petroglyphs.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a><figcaption>Petroglyphs of local <a href="/wiki/Urial" title="Urial">sheep</a>, Sary Kamysh</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">Kyrgyz</a> state reached its greatest expansion after defeating the <a href="/wiki/Uyghur_Khaganate" title="Uyghur Khaganate">Uyghur Khaganate</a> in 840 AD.<sup id="cite_ref-Kyrgyzstan_timeline_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kyrgyzstan_timeline-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> From the tenth century the Kyrgyz migrated as far as the <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan" title="Tian Shan">Tian Shan</a> range and maintained their dominance over this territory for about 200 years.
</p><p>There is a storytelling tradition of the <i><a href="/wiki/Epic_of_Manas" title="Epic of Manas">Epic of Manas</a></i>, which involves a warrior who unified all of the scattered tribes into a single nation in the 9th century. The trilogy, an element of the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_List" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage List">UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage List</a>, expresses the memory of the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">nomadic peoples</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the 12th century, the Kyrgyz dominion had shrunk to the <a href="/wiki/Altay_Mountains" class="mw-redirect" title="Altay Mountains">Altay</a> Range and <a href="/wiki/Sayan_Mountains" title="Sayan Mountains">Sayan Mountains</a> as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Mongols_before_Genghis_Khan" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongols before Genghis Khan">Mongol expansion</a>. With the rise of the <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a> in the thirteenth century, the Kyrgyz migrated south. The Kyrgyz peacefully became a part of the Mongol Empire in 1207.
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/220px-Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="331" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/330px-Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/440px-Caravanserai_Tash_Rabat_on_Silk_Road_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="3008" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Silk_road" class="mw-redirect" title="Silk road">Silk road</a> <a href="/wiki/Caravansarai" class="mw-redirect" title="Caravansarai">caravansarai</a> utilized during the <a href="/wiki/Islamic_Golden_Age" title="Islamic Golden Age">Islamic Golden Age</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Issyk_Kul_Lake" class="mw-redirect" title="Issyk Kul Lake">Issyk Kul Lake</a> was a stopover on the <a href="/wiki/Silk_Road" title="Silk Road">Silk Road</a>, a land route for traders, merchants, and other travelers from the Far East to Europe.
Kyrgyz tribes were overrun in the 17th century by the Mongols, in the mid-18th century by the <a href="/wiki/Manchu_people" title="Manchu people">Manchu</a>-led <a href="/wiki/Qing_dynasty" title="Qing dynasty">Qing dynasty</a> of China, and in the early 19th century by the Uzbek <a href="/wiki/Khanate_of_Kokand" title="Khanate of Kokand">Khanate of Kokand</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In 1842, the Kyrgyz tribes broke away from Kokand and united into the <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%9A%D1%8B%D1%80%D0%B3%D1%8B%D0%B7_%D1%85%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B4%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%8B&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Кыргыз хандыгы (page does not exist)">Kara-Kyrgyz Khanate</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ky.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9A%D1%8B%D1%80%D0%B3%D1%8B%D0%B7_%D1%85%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B4%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%8B" class="extiw" title="ky:Кыргыз хандыгы">ky</a>&#93;</span>, led by <a href="/wiki/Ormon_Khan" title="Ormon Khan">Ormon Khan</a>. Following Ormon's death in 1854, the khanate disintegrated.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Russian_conquest">Russian conquest</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Russian conquest">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Group_of_Kirghiz_(i.e._Kazakh)_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor,_his_wife,_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg/220px-Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg/330px-Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg/440px-Group_of_Kirghiz_%28i.e._Kazakh%29_men_posing_with_a_local_Russian_Governor%2C_his_wife%2C_and_their_child_in_front_of_a_yurt_LCCN99615493.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5120" data-file-height="3829" /></a><figcaption>Group of Kirghiz men posing with a local Russian Governor, his wife, and their child in front of a <a href="/wiki/Yurt" title="Yurt">yurt</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>In the late nineteenth century, the eastern part of what is today Kyrgyzstan, mainly the <a href="/wiki/Issyk-Kul_Region" title="Issyk-Kul Region">Issyk-Kul Region</a>, was ceded to the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a> by <a href="/wiki/Qing_dynasty" title="Qing dynasty">Qing China</a> through the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Tarbagatai" title="Treaty of Tarbagatai">Treaty of Tarbagatai</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The territory, then known in Russian as "Kirghizia", was formally incorporated into the Empire in 1876. The Russian takeover was met with numerous revolts, and many of the Kyrgyz opted to relocate to the <a href="/wiki/Pamir_Mountains" title="Pamir Mountains">Pamir Mountains</a> and <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>.
</p><p>In addition, the suppression of the <a href="/wiki/Central_Asian_revolt_of_1916" title="Central Asian revolt of 1916">1916 rebellion</a> against Russian rule in Central Asia caused many Kyrgyz later to migrate to China.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Since many ethnic groups in the region were, and still are, split between neighboring states at a time when borders were more porous and less regulated, it was common to move back and forth over the mountains, depending on where life was perceived as better; this might mean better rains for pasture or better government during oppression.
</p>
<h3><span id="Soviet_Kyrgyzstan_.281919.E2.80.931991.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Soviet_Kyrgyzstan_(1919–1991)">Soviet Kyrgyzstan (1919–1991)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Soviet Kyrgyzstan (1919–1991)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Soviet power was initially established in the region in 1919, and the <a href="/wiki/Kara-Kyrgyz_Autonomous_Oblast" class="mw-redirect" title="Kara-Kyrgyz Autonomous Oblast">Kara-Kyrgyz Autonomous Oblast</a> was created within the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Soviet_Federative_Socialist_Republic" title="Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic">Russian Soviet Federative Socialist Republic</a> (SFSR). The phrase Kara-Kirghiz was used until the mid-1920s by the Russians to distinguish them from the <a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakhs</a>, who were also referred to as Kirghiz. On 5 December 1936, the <a href="/wiki/Kirghiz_Soviet_Socialist_Republic" title="Kirghiz Soviet Socialist Republic">Kirghiz Soviet Socialist Republic</a> was established as a constituent <a href="/wiki/Republics_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Republics of the Soviet Union">Union Republic</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>.
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Civil_War" title="Russian Civil War">Russian Civil War</a>, the period of the <a href="/wiki/New_Economic_Policy" title="New Economic Policy">New Economic Policy</a> (NEP), began, which lasted roughly to 1928.<sup id="cite_ref-Thomas_1_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thomas_1-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Bolsheviks" title="Bolsheviks">Bolsheviks</a> made an effort to establish a standardized tax system, with higher taxes for nomads to discourage the wandering livelihood and they divided the Central Asia region into five nation-states.<sup id="cite_ref-Thomas_1_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thomas_1-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Edgar_2004_296_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edgar_2004_296-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Kyrgyzstan developed considerably in cultural, educational, and social life, literacy was greatly improved. Economic and social development also was notable.<sup id="cite_ref-USSR_National_and_Language_Policies_in_the_Early_Period_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USSR_National_and_Language_Policies_in_the_Early_Period-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Under <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Stalin" title="Joseph Stalin">Stalin</a> a great focus was put on Kyrgyz <a href="/wiki/Korenizatsiya" class="mw-redirect" title="Korenizatsiya">national identity</a>, the Soviet state was fighting tribalism as its social organization based on patrilineal kinship contradicted the concept of the modern nation state.<sup id="cite_ref-USSR_National_and_Language_Policies_in_the_Early_Period_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USSR_National_and_Language_Policies_in_the_Early_Period-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> From the indigenous perspective described as a difficult and ambivalent process of nation-building, in a region that did not know national institutions or consciousness before.<sup id="cite_ref-Edgar_2004_296_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edgar_2004_296-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By the end of the 1920s, the Soviet Union developed a series of <a href="/wiki/Five-year_plans_for_the_national_economy_of_the_Soviet_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Five-year plans for the national economy of the Soviet Union">five-year plans</a>, centered around <a href="/wiki/Industrialization_in_the_Soviet_Union" title="Industrialization in the Soviet Union">industrialization</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Collectivization_in_the_Soviet_Union" title="Collectivization in the Soviet Union">collectivization of agriculture</a>, including the creation of huge "<a href="/wiki/Kolkhoz" title="Kolkhoz">kolkhoz</a>" <a href="/wiki/Collective_farming" title="Collective farming">collective farming</a> systems, needed to feed the new workers in the industries.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Because of the plan's reliance on rapidity, major economic and cultural changes had to occur, which led to conflicts. In Kyrgyzstan, Russian settlers acquired the best pasture land, creating much hardship for most of its original inhabitants, <a href="/wiki/Kazakhs" title="Kazakhs">Kazakh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">Kyrgyz</a> and <a href="/wiki/Turkmens" title="Turkmens">Turkmen</a> nomads, who were also forced to settle down on soil that hadn't enough agricultural potential.<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_1_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica_1-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
<sup id="cite_ref-Edgar_2004_296_34-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edgar_2004_296-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The changes caused unrest, and between 1928 and 1932, nomads and peasants made it clear through methods like passive resistance that they did not agree with these policies, in the Kirgiziya area also guerrilla opposition occurred.<sup id="cite_ref-Edgar_2004_296_34-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Edgar_2004_296-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Thomas_1_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thomas_1-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_1_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica_1-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> The region suffered relatively more deaths from collectivization than any other.<sup id="cite_ref-Thomas_1_32-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thomas_1-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Suusamyr_Valley_(3968060227).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg/220px-Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg/330px-Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg/440px-Suusamyr_Valley_%283968060227%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="749" /></a><figcaption>Nomadic farming in the <a href="/wiki/Suusamyr_Valley" title="Suusamyr Valley">Suusamyr Valley</a>, Kyrgyzstan</figcaption></figure>
<p>The early years of <a href="/wiki/Glasnost" title="Glasnost">glasnost</a>, in the late 1980s, had little effect on the political climate in Kyrgyzstan. However, the Republic's press was permitted to adopt a more liberal stance and to establish a new publication, <i>Literaturny Kirghizstan</i>, by the Union of Writers. Unofficial political groups were forbidden, but several groups that emerged in 1989 to deal with the acute housing crisis were permitted to function.
</p><p>According to the last Soviet census in 1989, ethnic Kyrgyz made up only 22% of the residents of the northern city of <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Frunze</a> (now Bishkek), while more than 60% were Russians, Ukrainians, and people from other <a href="/wiki/Slavic_peoples" class="mw-redirect" title="Slavic peoples">Slavic</a> nations. Nearly 10% of the capital's population were Jewish (a rather unique fact, for almost any place in the Soviet Union, except the <a href="/wiki/Jewish_Autonomous_Oblast" title="Jewish Autonomous Oblast">Jewish Autonomous Oblast</a>).
</p><p>In June 1990, ethnic tensions between <a href="/wiki/Uzbeks" title="Uzbeks">Uzbeks</a> and Kyrgyz surfaced in the <a href="/wiki/Osh_Region" title="Osh Region">Osh Region</a> (southern Kyrgyzstan), where Uzbeks form a minority of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-conflict_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-conflict-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> The tensions between Kyrgyzs and Uzbeks in Osis led to 186 deaths.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Attempts to appropriate Uzbek <a href="/wiki/Collective_farming" title="Collective farming">collective farms</a> for housing development triggered the <a href="/wiki/Osh_riots_(1990)" class="mw-redirect" title="Osh riots (1990)">Osh Riots</a>. A state of emergency and curfew were introduced<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Askar_Akayev" title="Askar Akayev">Askar Akayev</a>, the youngest of five sons born into a family of collective farm workers (in northern Kyrgyzstan), was elected president in October of that same year. 
By then, the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Movement_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Democratic Movement of Kyrgyzstan">Kyrgyzstan Democratic Movement</a> (KDM) had developed into a significant political force with support in Parliament. On 15 December 1990, the Supreme Soviet voted to change the republic's name to the Republic of Kyrgyzstan. The following January, Akayev introduced new government structures and appointed a new cabinet composed mainly of younger, reform-oriented politicians. In February 1991, the name of the capital, Frunze, was changed back to its pre-revolutionary name of Bishkek.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite these political moves toward independence, economic realities seemed to work against secession from the Soviet Union. In a referendum on the preservation of the Soviet Union in March 1991, 88.7%<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> of the voters approved the proposal to retain the Soviet Union as a "renewed federation". Nevertheless, secessionist forces pushed Kyrgyzstan's independence through in August of that same year.
</p><p>On 19 August 1991, when the <a href="/wiki/State_Committee_on_the_State_of_Emergency" title="State Committee on the State of Emergency">State Emergency Committee</a> assumed power in Moscow, there was an attempt to depose Akayev in Kyrgyzstan. After the coup collapsed the following week, Akayev and Vice President <a href="/wiki/German_Kuznetsov" title="German Kuznetsov">German Kuznetsov</a> announced their resignations from the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Communist Party of the Soviet Union">Communist Party of the Soviet Union</a> (CPSU), and the entire bureau and secretariat resigned. This was followed by the Supreme Soviet vote declaring independence from the Soviet Union on 31 August 1991 as the <i>Republic of Kyrgyzstan</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_2_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica_2-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan,_2011-05.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg/220px-Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg/330px-Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg/440px-Stamps_of_Kyrgyzstan%2C_2011-05.jpg 2x" data-file-width="945" data-file-height="709" /></a><figcaption>Wild sheep, <a href="/wiki/Urial" title="Urial">urial</a>, on a Kyrgyzstan stamp</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to a 2013 <a href="/wiki/Gallup_(company)" class="mw-redirect" title="Gallup (company)">Gallup</a> poll, 62% of <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">Kyrgyz people</a> say that the collapse of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> harmed their country, while only 16% said that the collapse benefitted it.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In October 1991, Akayev ran unopposed and was elected president of the new independent Republic by direct ballot, receiving 95 percent of the votes cast. Together with the representatives of seven other Republics that same month, he signed the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Treaty_of_the_Economic_Community&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Treaty of the Economic Community (page does not exist)">Treaty of the Economic Community</a>. The new leaders of three out of four Soviet Union's founding republics, Russia, Belarus and Ukraine, on 8 December 1991 signed the <a href="/wiki/Belavezha_Accords" class="mw-redirect" title="Belavezha Accords">Belavezha Accords</a>, denouncing the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Creation_of_the_USSR" class="mw-redirect" title="Treaty on the Creation of the USSR">Union Treaty of 1922</a>, declaring that the Union would cease to exist and proclaimed the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Independent_States" title="Commonwealth of Independent States">Commonwealth of Independent States</a> (CIS) in its place.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 December 1991, Kyrgyzstan agreed with the other four Central Asian Republics, <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turkmenistan" title="Turkmenistan">Turkmenistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a> to the  <a href="/wiki/Alma-Ata_Protocol" title="Alma-Ata Protocol">Alma-Ata Protocols</a>, formally entering the Commonwealth with <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Azerbaijan" title="Azerbaijan">Azerbaijan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moldova" title="Moldova">Moldova</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukraine</a>. Finally, Kyrgyzstan gained full independence on 25 December 1991. The following day, on 26 December 1991, the Soviet Union ceased to exist. In 1992, Kyrgyzstan joined the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> (OSCE). On 5 May 1993, the official name changed from the Republic of Kyrgyzstan to the Kyrgyz Republic following the adoption of a new <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Constitution of Kyrgyzstan">constitution</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2005, an uprising known as the "<a href="/wiki/Tulip_Revolution" title="Tulip Revolution">Tulip Revolution</a>", took place after the parliamentary elections in March 2005, forced President <a href="/wiki/Askar_Akayev" title="Askar Akayev">Askar Akayev</a>'s resignation on 4 April 2005. Opposition leaders formed a coalition, and a new government was formed under President <a href="/wiki/Kurmanbek_Bakiyev" title="Kurmanbek Bakiyev">Kurmanbek Bakiyev</a> and Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Felix_Kulov" title="Felix Kulov">Felix Kulov</a>. The nation's capital was looted during the protests.
</p><p>Political stability appeared to be elusive, however, as various groups and factions allegedly linked to <a href="/wiki/Organized_crime" title="Organized crime">organized crime</a> jockeyed for power. Three of the 75 members of Parliament elected in March 2005 were assassinated, and another member was assassinated on 10 May 2006 shortly after winning his murdered brother's seat in a by-election. All four are reputed to have been directly involved in major illegal business ventures.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Words_to_watch#Unsupported_attributions" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Words to watch"><span title="The material near this tag may use weasel words or too-vague attribution. (December 2014)">according to whom?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="April_2010_crisis">April 2010 crisis</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: April 2010 crisis">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 6 April 2010, <a href="/wiki/2010_Kyrgyzstan_revolution" class="mw-redirect" title="2010 Kyrgyzstan revolution">civil unrest broke out</a> in the town of <a href="/wiki/Talas,_Kyrgyzstan" title="Talas, Kyrgyzstan">Talas</a> after a demonstration against government corruption and increased living expenses. The protests became violent, spreading to Bishkek by the following day. Protesters attacked President Bakiyev's offices, as well as state-run radio and television stations. There were conflicting reports that Interior Minister <a href="/wiki/Moldomusa_Kongatiyev" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldomusa Kongatiyev">Moldomusa Kongatiyev</a> had been beaten. On 7 April 2010, President Bakiyev imposed a state of emergency. Police and special services arrested many opposition leaders. In response, protesters took control of the internal security headquarters (former <a href="/wiki/KGB" title="KGB">KGB</a> headquarters) and a state television channel in the capital, Bishkek.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Reports by Kyrgyzstan government officials indicated that at least 75 people were killed and 458 hospitalized in bloody clashes with police in the capital.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Reports say that at least 80 people died as a result of clashes with police.
</p><p>A transition government has been established, led by former foreign minister <a href="/wiki/Roza_Otunbayeva" title="Roza Otunbayeva">Roza Otunbayeva</a> (<a href="/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Social Democratic Party of Kyrgyzstan">Social Democratic Party of Kyrgyzstan</a>), that by 8 April 2010 had taken control of <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Kyrgyzstan" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Kyrgyzstan">state media</a> and government facilities in the capital, but Bakiyev had not resigned from office.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>President Bakiyev returned to his home in <a href="/wiki/Jalal-Abad" title="Jalal-Abad">Jalal-Abad</a> and stated his terms of resignation at a press conference on 13 April 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> On 15 April 2010, Bakiyev left the country and flew to neighboring <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>, along with his wife and two children. The country's provisional leaders announced that Bakiyev signed a formal letter of resignation prior to his departure.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Daniar_Usenov" title="Daniar Usenov">Daniar Usenov</a> accused Russia of supporting the protests; this accusation was denied by Russian Prime Minister, <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a>. Opposition members also called for the closing of the US-controlled <a href="/wiki/Manas_Air_Base" class="mw-redirect" title="Manas Air Base">Manas Air Base</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Russia's President <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev" title="Dmitry Medvedev">Dmitry Medvedev</a> ordered measures to ensure the safety of Russian nationals and tighten security around Russian sites in Kyrgyzstan to protect them against possible attacks.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/2010_South_Kyrgyzstan_ethnic_clashes" title="2010 South Kyrgyzstan ethnic clashes">2010 South Kyrgyzstan ethnic clashes</a> occurred between the two main ethnic groups—the Uzbeks and Kyrgyz—in <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a>, the second-largest city in the country, on 11 June 2010. The clashes incited fears that the country could be heading towards a civil war.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Interim leader Otunbayeva sent a letter to the Russian president, Dimitry Medvedev, asking him to send Russian troops to help the country control the situation. Medvedev's Press Attaché, <a href="/wiki/Natalya_Timakova" title="Natalya Timakova">Natalya Timakova</a>, said in a reply to the letter, "It is an internal conflict and for now Russia does not see the conditions for taking part in its resolution". The clashes caused a shortage of food and other essential commodities with more than 200 killed and 1,685 people hurt, as of 12&#160;June&#160;2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>. The Russian government, however, said it would be sending humanitarian aid to the troubled nation.<sup id="cite_ref-By2010_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-By2010-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to local sources, there was a clash between two local gangs and it did not take long for the violence to spread to the rest of the city. There were also reports that the armed forces supported ethnic Kyrgyz gangs entering the city, but the government denied the allegations.<sup id="cite_ref-By2010_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-By2010-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The riots spread to neighboring areas, and the government declared a state of emergency in the entire southern Jalal-Abad region. To control the situation, the interim government gave special shoot-to-kill powers to the security forces. The Russian government decided to send a battalion to the country to protect Russian facilities.<sup id="cite_ref-By_the_BBC_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-By_the_BBC-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg/220px-Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg/330px-Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg/440px-Kyrgyz_family_Sary-Mogol.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1019" data-file-height="693" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyz family in the village of Sary-Mogol, <a href="/wiki/Osh_Region" title="Osh Region">Osh Region</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Otunbayeva accused the family of Bakiyev of "instigating the riots".<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> AFP reported "a veil of smoke covering the whole city". Authorities in neighboring <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a> said at least 30,000 Uzbeks had crossed the border to escape the riots.<sup id="cite_ref-By_the_BBC_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-By_the_BBC-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Osh became relatively calm on 14 June 2010, but Jalal-Abad witnessed sporadic incidents of arson. The entire region was still under a state of emergency as Uzbeks were reluctant to leave their houses for fear of attacks by the mobs. The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> decided to send an envoy to assess the situation.<sup id="cite_ref-news.bbc.co.uk_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-news.bbc.co.uk-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh,_Kyrgyzstan_(43654601644).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg/220px-From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg/330px-From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg/440px-From_Sary_Tash_to_Osh%2C_Kyrgyzstan_%2843654601644%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3194" data-file-height="2192" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyzstan's second-largest city, <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a>, in 2018</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Temir_Sariyev" title="Temir Sariyev">Temir Sariyev</a>, deputy chief of the interim government, said there were local clashes and that it was not possible [for the government] to fully control the situation. He added that there were not sufficient security forces to contain the violence. Media agencies reported on 14 June 2010 that the Russian government was considering a request by the Kyrgyz government. An emergency meeting of the <a href="/wiki/Collective_Security_Treaty_Organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Collective Security Treaty Organisation">Collective Security Treaty Organisation</a> (CSTO) was held on the same day (14 June) to discuss the role it could play in helping to end the violence.
Ethnic violence waned, according to the Kyrgyz government, by 15 June 2010 and Kyrgyz president Roza Otunbayeva held a news conference that day and declared that there was no need for Russia to send in troops to quell the violence. There were at least 170 people left dead by 15 June 2010 but Pascale Meige Wagner of the <a href="/wiki/International_Committee_of_the_Red_Cross" title="International Committee of the Red Cross">International Committee of the Red Cross</a> said the [official] death toll was an underestimate. The UN High Commissioner told reporters in <a href="/wiki/Geneva" title="Geneva">Geneva</a> that evidence suggested that the violence seemed to have been staged up.
Ethnic Uzbeks threatened to blow up an oil depot in Osh if they failed to get guarantees of protection. The United Nations said it believed that the attacks were "orchestrated, targeted and well-planned". Kyrgyz officials told the media that a person suspected to be behind the violence in Jalal-Abad had been detained.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_2010-06-16_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_2010-06-16-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 2 August 2010, a Kyrgyz government commission began investigating the causes of the clashes. Members of the National Commission, led by former parliament speaker Abdygany Erkebaev, met with people from the predominantly ethnic Uzbek villages of Mady, Shark, and Kyzyl-Kyshtak in the Kara-Suu district of Osh Oblast. This National Commission, including representatives of many ethnic groups, was established by a presidential decree.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg/220px-%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg/330px-%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg/440px-%D0%9F%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%9A%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B3%D0%B8%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%B8_%D0%B8_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D0%B4%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%BB%D1%8C_%D0%9D%D0%90%D0%91%D0%A3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="924" /></a><figcaption>President <a href="/wiki/Roza_Otunbayeva" title="Roza Otunbayeva">Otunbayeva</a> meets representatives of an environmental organisation in 2011.</figcaption></figure>
<p>President Roza Otunbayeva also said in August 2010 that an international commission would be formed to investigate the clashes.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The international commission conducted an extensive investigation and prepared a report—The Independent international commission of inquiry into the events in southern Kyrgyzstan in June 2010 (KIC).<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> It stated that "The Provisional Government, which had assumed power two months before the events, either failed to recognize or underestimated the deterioration in inter-ethnic relations in southern Kyrgyzstan". The KIC concluded that the "Provisional Government had the responsibility to ensure that the security forces were adequately trained and appropriately equipped to deal with situations of civil unrest" but were unable to take necessary measures.
</p><p>As of today, Kyrgyzstan celebrates its Independence Day annually on August 31, the anniversary of its declaration of independence in 1991. Since independence, Kyrgyzstan has made developments such as creating genuinely free news media and fostering an active political opposition.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In late April 2021, a <a href="/wiki/Water_conflict" title="Water conflict">conflict over water</a> escalated into one of the most serious <a href="/wiki/2021_Kyrgyzstan%E2%80%93Tajikistan_conflict" class="mw-redirect" title="2021 Kyrgyzstan–Tajikistan conflict">border clashes</a> between Kyrgyzstan and <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a> since independence in 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2022 armed clashes, including the use of artillery, erupted along much of the border between Kyrgyzstan and Tajikistan.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Geography of Kyrgyzstan">Geography of Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png/220px-Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png/330px-Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png/440px-Kyrgyzstan_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="1705" data-file-height="1015" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyzstan's topography</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bringing_the_sheep_home,_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_(3968109583).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg/220px-Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg/330px-Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg/440px-Bringing_the_sheep_home%2C_on_the_southern_shore_of_Issyk-Kul_%283968109583%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="900" /></a><figcaption>On the southern shore of Issyk Kul lake, <a href="/wiki/Issyk_Kul_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Issyk Kul Region">Issyk Kul Region</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Kyrgyzstan is a landlocked country in <a href="/wiki/Central_Asia" title="Central Asia">Central Asia</a>, bordering <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>, China, <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a>. It lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/39th_parallel_north" title="39th parallel north">39°</a> and <a href="/wiki/44th_parallel_north" title="44th parallel north">44° N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/69th_meridian_east" title="69th meridian east">69°</a> and <a href="/wiki/81st_meridian_east" title="81st meridian east">81° E</a>. It is farther from the sea than any other individual country, and all its rivers flow into <a href="/wiki/Endorheic_basin" title="Endorheic basin">closed drainage systems which do not reach the sea</a>. The mountainous region of the <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan" title="Tian Shan">Tian Shan</a> covers over 80% of the country (Kyrgyzstan is occasionally referred to as "the Switzerland of Central Asia", as a result),<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> with the remainder made up of valleys and basins.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyzstan.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Kyrgyzstan.png/330px-Kyrgyzstan.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Kyrgyzstan.png/495px-Kyrgyzstan.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/ce/Kyrgyzstan.png 2x" data-file-width="633" data-file-height="321" /></a><figcaption>A map of Kyrgyzstan</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Issyk-Kul_Lake" class="mw-redirect" title="Issyk-Kul Lake">Issyk-Kul Lake</a>, or Ysyk-Köl in <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_Language" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyz Language">Kyrgyz</a>, in the north-eastern Tian Shan is the largest lake in Kyrgyzstan and the second largest mountain lake in the world after <a href="/wiki/Titicaca" class="mw-redirect" title="Titicaca">Titicaca</a>. The lowest point is in <a href="/wiki/Kara_Darya" title="Kara Darya">Kara-Daryya</a> (Karadar'ya) at 132 meters and the highest peaks are in the Kakshaal-Too range, forming the Chinese border. Peak <a href="/wiki/Jengish_Chokusu" title="Jengish Chokusu">Jengish Chokusu</a>, at 7,439&#160;m (24,406&#160;ft), is the highest point and is considered by geologists to be the northernmost peak over 7,000&#160;m (22,966&#160;ft) in the world. Heavy snowfall in winter leads to spring floods which often cause serious damage downstream. The runoff from the mountains is also used for <a href="/wiki/Hydro-electricity" class="mw-redirect" title="Hydro-electricity">hydro-electricity</a>.
</p><p>Kyrgyzstan has significant deposits of metals including <a href="/wiki/Gold" title="Gold">gold</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rare-earth_metals" class="mw-redirect" title="Rare-earth metals">rare-earth metals</a>. Due to the country's predominantly mountainous terrain, less than 8% of the land is cultivated, and this is concentrated in the northern lowlands and the fringes of the <a href="/wiki/Fergana_Valley" title="Fergana Valley">Fergana Valley</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a> in the north is the capital and largest city, with 937,400 inhabitants (as of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>). The second city is the ancient town of <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a>, located in the Fergana Valley near the border with Uzbekistan. The principal river is the <a href="/wiki/Kara_Darya" title="Kara Darya">Kara Darya</a>, which flows west through the Fergana Valley into Uzbekistan. Across the border in Uzbekistan it meets another major Kyrgyz river, the <a href="/wiki/Naryn_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Naryn River">Naryn</a>.
</p><p>The confluence forms the <a href="/wiki/Syr_Darya" title="Syr Darya">Syr Darya</a>, which originally flowed into the <a href="/wiki/Aral_Sea" title="Aral Sea">Aral Sea</a>. As of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, it no longer reaches the sea, as its water is withdrawn upstream to irrigate cotton fields in <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a>, and southern <a href="/wiki/Kazakhstan" title="Kazakhstan">Kazakhstan</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Chu_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Chu River">Chu River</a> also briefly flows through Kyrgyzstan before entering Kazakhstan.
</p><p>Kyrgyzstan contains seven terrestrial ecosystems: <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan_montane_conifer_forests" title="Tian Shan montane conifer forests">Tian Shan montane conifer forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alai-Western_Tian_Shan_steppe" class="mw-redirect" title="Alai-Western Tian Shan steppe">Alai-Western Tian Shan steppe</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gissaro-Alai_open_woodlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Gissaro-Alai open woodlands">Gissaro-Alai open woodlands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan_foothill_arid_steppe" title="Tian Shan foothill arid steppe">Tian Shan foothill arid steppe</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pamir_alpine_desert_and_tundra" title="Pamir alpine desert and tundra">Pamir alpine desert and tundra</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan_montane_steppe_and_meadows" title="Tian Shan montane steppe and meadows">Tian Shan montane steppe and meadows</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Central_Asian_northern_desert" title="Central Asian northern desert">Central Asian northern desert</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 8.86/10, ranking it 13th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Kyrgyzstan" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Kyrgyzstan">Climate of Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg/310px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg/465px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg/620px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_KGZ_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1769" data-file-height="850" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyzstan map of Köppen climate classification</figcaption></figure>
<p>The climate varies regionally. The low-lying <a href="/wiki/Fergana_Valley" title="Fergana Valley">Fergana Valley</a> in the southwest is <a href="/wiki/Subtropical_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Subtropical climate">subtropical</a> and extremely hot in <a href="/wiki/Summer" title="Summer">summer</a>, with temperatures reaching 40&#160;°C (104&#160;°F) The northern <a href="/wiki/Foothill" class="mw-redirect" title="Foothill">foothills</a> are <a href="/wiki/Temperate_climate" title="Temperate climate">temperate</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Tian_Shan" title="Tian Shan">Tian Shan</a> varies from dry <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">continental</a> to <a href="/wiki/Polar_climate" title="Polar climate">polar climate</a>, depending on elevation. In the coldest areas temperatures are sub-zero for around 40 days in winter, and even some <a href="/wiki/Desert" title="Desert">desert</a> areas experience constant snowfall in this period. In the lowlands the temperature ranges from around −6&#160;°C (21&#160;°F) in January to 24&#160;°C (75&#160;°F) in July.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Glaciers_and_Climate_change">Glaciers and Climate change</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Glaciers and Climate change">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Climate change in Kyrgyzstan">Climate change in Kyrgyzstan</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf/page1-220px-Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf/page1-330px-Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf/page1-440px-Climate_change_Kyrgyzstan.pdf.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1650" data-file-height="1275" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Retreat_of_glaciers_since_1850" title="Retreat of glaciers since 1850">Reduction of glacier area</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Borkoldoy_Too" title="Borkoldoy Too">Borkoldoy</a> range (1965-2010)</figcaption></figure><a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Climate change in Kyrgyzstan">Climate change in Kyrgyzstan</a> is already having impacts. Among the countries in <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern Europe</a> and <a href="/wiki/Central_Asia" title="Central Asia">Central Asia</a>, Kyrgyzstan is the third most vulnerable to the <a href="/wiki/Effects_of_climate_change" title="Effects of climate change">effects of climate change</a>, such as changes in weather patterns that could lead to prolonged periods of precipitation and drought.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>  Moreover, the frequent occurrence of extreme weather and climate events such as heatwaves, droughts, heavy rainfalls, and biotic/abiotic catastrophes during recent years are evidence of climate change.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>  Kyrgyzstan is threatened with glacier melting and a lack of freshwater balance, which are accelerated by global warming.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Their average temperature has increased from 5.8&#160;°C to 6&#160;°C so far within the last 20 years.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan_:22_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan_:22-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> In 2013 the World Bank estimated a likely increase of 2&#160;°C in average mean temperature by 2060 and of 4–5&#160;°C by 2100, noting that the country's glaciers were significantly reduced and projected to decline further.<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan_:14_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_Kyrgyzstan_:14-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> However the very slight increase in temperature is expected to positively affect climate-sensitive sectors such as agriculture, energy, and forestry as more land is within the optimum temperature band.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Enclaves_and_exclaves">Enclaves and exclaves</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Enclaves and exclaves">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There is one <a href="/wiki/Enclave_and_exclave" title="Enclave and exclave">exclave</a>, the tiny village of <a href="/wiki/Barak,_Kyrgyzstan" title="Barak, Kyrgyzstan">Barak</a><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> (population 627), in the <a href="/wiki/Fergana_Valley" title="Fergana Valley">Fergana Valley</a>. The village is surrounded by <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbek</a> territory. It is located on the road from <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a> (Kyrgyzstan) to Khodjaabad (Uzbekistan) about 4 kilometres (2 miles) north-west from the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz%E2%80%93Uzbek_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyz–Uzbek border">Kyrgyz–Uzbek border</a> in the direction of <a href="/wiki/Andijan" title="Andijan">Andijan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Barak is administratively part of <a href="/wiki/Kara-Suu_District" title="Kara-Suu District">Kara-Suu District</a> in Kyrgyzstan's <a href="/wiki/Osh_Region" title="Osh Region">Osh Region</a>.
</p><p>There are four Uzbek <a href="/wiki/Enclave_and_exclave" title="Enclave and exclave">enclaves</a> within Kyrgyzstan. Two of them are the towns of <a href="/wiki/Sokh_District" title="Sokh District">Sokh</a>, with an area of 325&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (125&#160;sq&#160;mi) and a population of 42,800 in 1993—although some estimates go as high as 70,000 (99% are <a href="/wiki/Tajik_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Tajik people">Tajiks</a>, the remainder <a href="/wiki/Uzbeks" title="Uzbeks">Uzbeks</a>); and <a href="/wiki/Shohimardon" title="Shohimardon">Shakhimardan</a> (also known as Shahimardan, Shohimardon, or Shah-i-Mardan, area 90&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (35&#160;sq&#160;mi) and a population of 5,100 in 1993; 91% are Uzbeks, the remainder <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_people" title="Kyrgyz people">Kyrgyz</a>); the other two are the tiny territories of Chong-Kara (roughly 3&#160;km (2&#160;mi) long by 1&#160;km (0.6&#160;mi) wide) and Jangy-ayyl (a dot of land barely 2–3&#160;km (1–2&#160;mi) across). Chong-Kara is on the <a href="/wiki/Sokh_river" class="mw-redirect" title="Sokh river">Sokh river</a>, between the Uzbek border and the Sokh enclave. Jangy-ayyl is about 60 kilometres (37&#160;mi) east of <a href="/wiki/Batken" title="Batken">Batken</a>, in a northward projection of the Kyrgyz-Uzbek border near <a href="/wiki/Khalmion" class="mw-redirect" title="Khalmion">Khalmion</a>.
</p><p>There are also two enclaves belonging to <a href="/wiki/Tajikistan" title="Tajikistan">Tajikistan</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz-Tajik_border" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyz-Tajik border">Kyrgyz-Tajik border</a>: <a href="/wiki/Vorukh" title="Vorukh">Vorukh</a> (exclave area between 95–130&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (37–50&#160;sq&#160;mi), population estimated between 23,000 and 29,000, 95% Tajiks and 5% Kyrgyz, distributed among 17 villages), located 45 kilometres (28&#160;mi) south of <a href="/wiki/Isfara" title="Isfara">Isfara</a> on the right bank of the river <a href="/wiki/Karavshin" class="mw-redirect" title="Karavshin">Karavshin</a>, and a small settlement <a href="/wiki/Lolazor,_Jabbor_Rasulov_District" title="Lolazor, Jabbor Rasulov District">Lolazor</a> (Western Qalacha or Kayragach) near the Kyrgyz railway station of <a href="/wiki/Kairagach" class="mw-redirect" title="Kairagach">Kairagach</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Kyrgyzstan" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Kyrgyzstan">Government of Kyrgyzstan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Politics of Kyrgyzstan">Politics of Kyrgyzstan</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Foreign relations of Kyrgyzstan">Foreign relations of Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_system">Political system</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Political system">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_(05-11-2021).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg/220px-%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="307" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg/330px-%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg/440px-%D0%A1%D0%B0%D0%B4%D1%8B%D1%80_%D0%96%D0%B0%D0%BF%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B2_%2805-11-2021%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="774" data-file-height="1079" /></a><figcaption>Sadyr Japarov, <a href="/wiki/President_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="President of Kyrgyzstan">President of Kyrgyzstan</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bishkek_P9170503_192_(39204123095).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg/220px-Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg/330px-Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg/440px-Bishkek_P9170503_192_%2839204123095%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Supreme_Council_(Kyrgyzstan)" title="Supreme Council (Kyrgyzstan)">Supreme Council</a> building in <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a>, house of the <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral parliament</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg/220px-Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg/330px-Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg/440px-Kyrgyz_women_parliamentarians.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="675" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyz women parliamentarians, 2016</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/1993%E2%80%932010_Constitution_of_Kyrgyzstan" class="mw-redirect" title="1993–2010 Constitution of Kyrgyzstan">1993 constitution</a> defines the form of government as a democratic <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> republic. The executive branch includes a president and prime minister. The parliament currently is unicameral. The judicial branch comprises a supreme court, local courts and a chief prosecutor.
</p><p>In March 2002, in the southern district of <a href="/wiki/Aksy" class="mw-redirect" title="Aksy">Aksy</a>, five people protesting the <a href="/wiki/Arbitrary_arrest_and_detention" title="Arbitrary arrest and detention">arbitrary arrest</a> of an opposition politician were shot dead by police, sparking nationwide protests. President <a href="/wiki/Askar_Akayev" title="Askar Akayev">Askar Akayev</a> initiated a constitutional reform process which initially included the participation of a broad range of government, civil and social representatives in an open dialogue, leading to a February 2003 referendum marred by voting irregularities.
</p><p>The amendments to the constitution approved by the referendum resulted in stronger control by the president and weakened the parliament and the Constitutional Court. Parliamentary elections for a new, 75-seat unicameral legislature were held on 27 February and 13 March 2005, but were widely viewed as corrupt. The subsequent protests led to a bloodless <a href="/wiki/Coup" class="mw-redirect" title="Coup">coup</a> on 24 March 2005, after which Akayev fled the country with his family and was replaced by acting president <a href="/wiki/Kurmanbek_Bakiyev" title="Kurmanbek Bakiyev">Kurmanbek Bakiyev</a>.
</p><p>On 10 July 2005, acting president Bakiyev won the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_presidential_election,_2005" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyz presidential election, 2005">presidential election</a> in a landslide, with 88.9% of the vote, and was inaugurated on 14 August.
