<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Christianity in Kyrgyzstan">Christianity</a></td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:blue; width:7%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">7%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Religion in Kyrgyzstan">other</a></td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:grey; width:3%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">3%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Religion in Kyrgyzstan">Religion in Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg/220px-Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg/330px-Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg/440px-Karakol-Dungan-Mosque-Exterior-1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Karakol#Dungan_mosque" title="Karakol">Karakol Dungan Mosque</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> is the dominant religion of Kyrgyzstan. The CIA World Factbook estimates that as of 2017, 90% of the population is Muslim, with the majority being <a href="/wiki/Sunni" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni">Sunni</a>; 7% are Christian, including 3% <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodoxy</a>, and the remainder are other religions.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_Factbook_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_Factbook-139">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> A 2009 <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Research Center</a> report indicated 86.3% of Kyrgyzstan's population adhering to Islam.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The great majority of Muslims are Sunni, adhering to the <a href="/wiki/Hanafi" class="mw-redirect" title="Hanafi">Hanafi school</a> of thought,<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> although a 2012 Pew survey report showed that only 23% of respondents to a questionnaire chose to identify themselves as Sunni, with 64% volunteering that they were "just a Muslim".<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> There are a few <a href="/wiki/Ahmadiyya" title="Ahmadiyya">Ahmadiyya</a> Muslims, though unrecognised by the country.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Later figures show a rise in the number of agnostics in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During Soviet times, <a href="/wiki/State_atheism" title="State atheism">state atheism</a> was encouraged. Today, however, Kyrgyzstan is a <a href="/wiki/Secular_state" title="Secular state">secular state</a>, although Islam has exerted a growing influence in politics.<sup id="cite_ref-isn_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-isn-145">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> For instance, there has been an attempt to arrange for officials to travel on <i><a href="/wiki/Hajj" title="Hajj">hajj</a></i> (the pilgrimage to <a href="/wiki/Mecca" title="Mecca">Mecca</a>) under a tax-free arrangement.
</p><p>While <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Islam in Kyrgyzstan">Islam in Kyrgyzstan</a> is more of a cultural background than a devout daily practice for many, public figures have expressed support for restoring religious values. For example, <a href="/wiki/Human_rights" title="Human rights">human rights</a> <a href="/wiki/Ombudsman" title="Ombudsman">ombudsman</a> Tursunbay Bakir-Ulu noted, "In this era of independence, it is not surprising that there has been a return to spiritual roots not only in Kyrgyzstan, but also in other post-communist republics. It would be immoral to develop a market-based society without an ethical dimension."<sup id="cite_ref-isn_145-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-isn-145">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bishkek_church_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Bishkek_church_01.jpg/170px-Bishkek_church_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Bishkek_church_01.jpg/255px-Bishkek_church_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Bishkek_church_01.jpg/340px-Bishkek_church_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1932" data-file-height="2576" /></a><figcaption>Bishkek <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Additionally, <a href="/wiki/Bermet_Akayeva" title="Bermet Akayeva">Bermet Akayeva</a>, the daughter of <a href="/wiki/Askar_Akayev" title="Askar Akayev">Askar Akayev</a>, the former President of Kyrgyzstan, stated during a July 2007 interview that Islam is increasingly taking root across the nation.<sup id="cite_ref-EurasiaNet_Kyrgyzstan_Time_to_Ponder_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EurasiaNet_Kyrgyzstan_Time_to_Ponder-146">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> She emphasized that many <a href="/wiki/Mosque" title="Mosque">mosques</a> have recently been built and that the Kyrgyz are increasingly devoting themselves to Islam, which she noted was "not a bad thing in itself. It keeps our society more moral, cleaner."<sup id="cite_ref-EurasiaNet_Kyrgyzstan_Time_to_Ponder_146-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EurasiaNet_Kyrgyzstan_Time_to_Ponder-146">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> There is a contemporary <a href="/wiki/Sufi" class="mw-redirect" title="Sufi">Sufi</a> order present which adheres to a somewhat different form of Islam than the orthodox Islam.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/220px-Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/330px-Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg/440px-Mosque_under_construction_in_Kyrgyzstan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3500" data-file-height="2500" /></a><figcaption>Mosque under construction in Kyrgyzstan</figcaption></figure>
<p>The other faiths practiced in Kyrgyzstan include <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox</a> and <a href="/wiki/Orthodox_Church_of_Ukraine" title="Orthodox Church of Ukraine">Ukrainian Orthodox</a> versions of <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a>, practiced primarily by <a href="/wiki/Russians" title="Russians">Russians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ukrainians" title="Ukrainians">Ukrainians</a> respectively. A community of 5000 to 10,000 Jehovah's Witnesses gather in both Kyrgyz and Russian-speaking congregations, as well as some Chinese- and Turkish-speaking groups.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> A small minority of ethnic Germans are also Christian, mostly <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran Church">Lutheran</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anabaptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Anabaptist">Anabaptist</a> as well as a <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a> community of approximately 600.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A few <a href="/wiki/Animism" title="Animism">Animistic</a> traditions survive, as do influences from <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> such as the tying of <a href="/wiki/Prayer_flag" title="Prayer flag">prayer flags</a> onto sacred trees, though some view this practice rooted within <a href="/wiki/Sufi" class="mw-redirect" title="Sufi">Sufi</a> Islam.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> There is also a small number of <a href="/wiki/Bukharian_Jews" class="mw-redirect" title="Bukharian Jews">Bukharian Jews</a> living in Kyrgyzstan, but during the collapse of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> most fled to other countries, mainly the United States and Israel. In addition, there is a small community of <a href="/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews" title="Ashkenazi Jews">Ashkenazi Jews</a>, who fled to the country from eastern Europe during the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 6 November 2008, the Kyrgyzstan parliament unanimously passed a law increasing the minimum number of adherents for recognizing a religion from 10 to 200. It also outlawed "aggressive action aimed at <a href="/wiki/Proselytism" title="Proselytism">proselytism</a>", and banned religious activity in schools and all activity by unregistered organizations. It was signed by President <a href="/wiki/Kurmanbek_Bakiyev" title="Kurmanbek Bakiyev">Kurmanbek Bakiyev</a> on 12 January 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There have been several reported police raids against peaceful minority religious meetings,<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> as well as reports of officials planting false evidence,<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> but also some court decisions in favour of religious minorities.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre,_Bishkek.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg/220px-Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg/330px-Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg/440px-Kyrgyz_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre%2C_Bishkek.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a><figcaption>Kyrgyz Opera and Ballet Theatre, Bishkek</figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Culture of Kyrgyzstan">Culture of Kyrgyzstan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyz_cuisine" title="Kyrgyz cuisine">Kyrgyz cuisine</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Traditions">Traditions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Traditions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyz_Manaschi,_Karakol.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Kyrgyz_Manaschi%2C_Karakol.jpg/220px-Kyrgyz_Manaschi%2C_Karakol.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="292" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Kyrgyz_Manaschi%2C_Karakol.jpg/330px-Kyrgyz_Manaschi%2C_Karakol.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a4/Kyrgyz_Manaschi%2C_Karakol.jpg 2x" data-file-width="394" data-file-height="523" /></a><figcaption>A Kyrgyz <i>manaschi</i> performing part of the <i><a href="/wiki/Epic_of_Manas" title="Epic of Manas">Epic of Manas</a></i> at a yurt camp in <a href="/wiki/Karakol" title="Karakol">Karakol</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg/220px-Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg/330px-Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg/440px-Kyrgyz_Musicians_in_Karakol.jpg 2x" data-file-width="864" data-file-height="592" /></a><figcaption>Musicians playing traditional Kyrgyz music</figcaption></figure>
<ul><li><i><a href="/wiki/Manas_(epic)" class="mw-redirect" title="Manas (epic)">Manas</a></i>, an <a href="/wiki/Epic_poem" class="mw-redirect" title="Epic poem">epic poem</a>, the plot revolves around a series of events that coincide with the history of the region in the 9th century, primarily the interaction of the Kyrgyz people with other Turkic and Chinese people.</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Komuz" title="Komuz">Komuz</a></i>, a three-stringed lute</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Tush_kyiz" title="Tush kyiz">Tush kyiz</a></i>, large, elaborately embroidered wall hangings</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Shyrdak" title="Shyrdak">Shyrdak</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Ala-kiyiz" class="mw-redirect" title="Ala-kiyiz">Ala-kiyiz</a></i> carpets, manufactured by the process of <a href="/wiki/Felt" title="Felt">felting</a>, used for yurts. Inscribed in 2012 on the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists" title="UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage Lists">UNESCO List of Intangible Cultural Heritage in Need of Urgent Safeguarding</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Other <a href="/wiki/Textiles" class="mw-redirect" title="Textiles">textiles</a>, especially made from felt</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Ala_kachuu" title="Ala kachuu">Ala kachuu</a></i>, "bride kidnapping", traditional form of marriage in Kyrgyzstan</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Falconry" title="Falconry">Falconry</a></li></ul>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._(3968888530).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg/220px-Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg/330px-Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg/440px-Ishenbek_and_Berkut_scout_for_prey._%283968888530%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="900" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Hunting_with_eagles" title="Hunting with eagles">Hunting with an eagle</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Illegal, but still practiced, is the tradition of <a href="/wiki/Bride_kidnapping" title="Bride kidnapping">bride kidnapping</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> It is debatable whether bride kidnapping is actually traditional. Some of the confusion may stem from the fact that <a href="/wiki/Arranged_marriage" title="Arranged marriage">arranged marriages</a> were traditional, and one of the ways to escape an arranged marriage was to arrange a consensual "kidnapping".<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flag">Flag</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Flag">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The 40-rayed yellow sun in the center of <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Kyrgyzstan" title="Flag of Kyrgyzstan">the national flag</a> represent the 40 tribes that once made up the entirety of Kyrgyz culture before the intervention of Russia during the rise of the Soviet Union. The lines inside the sun represent the crown or tündük (Kyrgyz түндүк) of a yurt, a symbol replicated in many facets of Kyrgyz architecture. The red portion of the flag represents peace and openness of Kyrgyzstan.
</p><p>Under Soviet rule and before 1992, it had the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Flag of the Soviet Union">flag of the Soviet Union</a> with two big blue stripes and a white thin stripe in the middle.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_holidays">Public holidays</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Public holidays">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Public holidays in Kyrgyzstan">Public holidays in Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<p>In addition to celebrating the <a href="/wiki/New_Year" title="New Year">New Year</a> each 1 January, the Kyrgyz observe the traditional New Year festival <a href="/wiki/Nowruz" title="Nowruz">Nowruz</a> on the vernal equinox. This spring holiday is celebrated with feasts and festivities such as the horse game <a href="/wiki/Buzkashi" title="Buzkashi">Ulak Tartish</a>.
</p><p>This is the list of public holidays in Kyrgyzstan:
</p>
<ul><li>1 January – New Year's Day</li>
<li>7 January – Orthodox Christmas</li>
<li>23 February – Fatherland Defender's Day</li>
<li>8 March – Women's Day</li>
<li>21–23 March – <a href="/wiki/Nowruz" title="Nowruz">Nooruz Mairamy</a>, Persian New Year (spring festival)</li>
<li>7 April – Day of National Revolution</li>
<li>1 May – Labor Day</li>
<li>5 May – Constitution Day</li>
<li>8 May – Remembrance Day</li>
<li>9 May – <a href="/wiki/Victory_Day_(9_May)" title="Victory Day (9 May)">Victory Day</a></li>
<li>31 August – Independence Day</li>
<li>7–8 November – Days of History and Commemoration of Ancestors</li></ul>
<p>Two additional Muslim holidays <i><a href="/wiki/Eid_al-Fitr" title="Eid al-Fitr">Orozo Ayt</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Eid_al-Adha" title="Eid al-Adha">Qurman (or Qurban) Ayt</a></i> are defined by the lunar calendar.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Rugby union in Kyrgyzstan">Rugby union in Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG/220px-Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG/330px-Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG/440px-Bandy_2012._KYR_-_JPN.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4320" data-file-height="3240" /></a><figcaption>Bandy: Kyrgyzstan in red against Japan</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the most popular sport in Kyrgyzstan. The official governing body is the <a href="/wiki/Football_Federation_of_Kyrgyz_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Football Federation of Kyrgyz Republic">Football Federation of Kyrgyz Republic</a>, which was founded in 1992, after the split of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>. It administers the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan_national_football_team" title="Kyrgyzstan national football team">Kyrgyzstan national football team</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Wrestling" title="Wrestling">Wrestling</a> is also very popular. In the <a href="/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympic_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="2008 Summer Olympic Games">2008 Summer Olympic Games</a>, two athletes from Kyrgyzstan won medals in <a href="/wiki/Greco-Roman_wrestling" title="Greco-Roman wrestling">Greco-Roman wrestling</a>: <a href="/wiki/Kanatbek_Begaliev" title="Kanatbek Begaliev">Kanatbek Begaliev</a> (silver) and <a href="/wiki/Ruslan_Tyumenbayev" title="Ruslan Tyumenbayev">Ruslan Tyumenbayev</a> (bronze).<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ice_hockey" title="Ice hockey">Ice hockey</a> was not as popular in Kyrgyzstan until the first <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan_Championship_(ice_hockey)" title="Kyrgyzstan Championship (ice hockey)">Ice Hockey Championship</a> was organized in 2009. In 2011, the <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan_men%27s_national_ice_hockey_team" title="Kyrgyzstan men&#39;s national ice hockey team">Kyrgyzstan men's national ice hockey team</a> won <a href="/wiki/2011_Asian_Winter_Games" title="2011 Asian Winter Games">2011 Asian Winter Games</a> Premier Division dominating in all six games with six wins. It was the first major international event that Kyrgyzstan's ice hockey team took part in.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> The Kyrgyzstan men's ice hockey team joined the <a href="/wiki/IIHF" class="mw-redirect" title="IIHF">IIHF</a> in July 2011.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bandy" title="Bandy">Bandy</a> is becoming increasingly popular in the country. The Kyrgyz national team took Kyrgyzstan's first medal at the <a href="/wiki/Asian_Winter_Games" title="Asian Winter Games">Asian Winter Games</a>, when they captured the bronze. They played in the <a href="/wiki/Bandy_World_Championship_2012" class="mw-redirect" title="Bandy World Championship 2012">Bandy World Championship 2012</a>, their first appearance in that tournament.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Martial Arts: <a href="/wiki/Valentina_Shevchenko" title="Valentina Shevchenko">Valentina Shevchenko</a> is a Kyrgyzstani–<a href="/wiki/Peru" title="Peru">Peruvian</a> professional mixed martial artist who competes in the <a href="/wiki/Flyweight_(MMA)" class="mw-redirect" title="Flyweight (MMA)">women's flyweight</a> division of the <a href="/wiki/Ultimate_Fighting_Championship" title="Ultimate Fighting Championship">Ultimate Fighting Championship</a> (UFC), where she was formerly the <a href="/wiki/UFC_Women%27s_Flyweight_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="UFC Women&#39;s Flyweight Championship">Women's Flyweight champion</a>.
</p><p>Boxing: <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Bivol" title="Dmitry Bivol">Dmitry Bivol</a> is a Kyrgyzstani Professional Boxer from <a href="/wiki/Tokmok" title="Tokmok">Tokmok</a>, who competes in the Light Heavyweight Division. Since 2017, he has held the <a href="/wiki/World_Boxing_Association" title="World Boxing Association">World Boxing Association</a> <a href="/wiki/Light_Heavyweight" class="mw-redirect" title="Light Heavyweight">Light Heavyweight</a> Title. As of August 2019, Bivol is ranked as the world's best active light-heavyweight by the Transnational Boxing Rankings Board and BoxRec, and third by The <a href="/wiki/Ring_Magazine" class="mw-redirect" title="Ring Magazine">Ring Magazine</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan%27s_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyzstan&#39;s national basketball team">Kyrgyzstan's national basketball team</a> had its best performance at the official <a href="/wiki/1995_Asian_Basketball_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="1995 Asian Basketball Championship">1995 Asian Basketball Championship</a> where the team surprisingly finished ahead of favorites such as <a href="/wiki/Iran_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Iran national basketball team">Iran</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philippines_men%27s_national_basketball_team" title="Philippines men&#39;s national basketball team">Philippines</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jordan_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Jordan national basketball team">Jordan</a>.
</p><p>XXI International Issyk-Kul Sports Games (SCO + CIS) was held in 9–17 September 2022 in Baktuu-Dolonotu village (<a href="/wiki/Issyk-Kul" title="Issyk-Kul">Issyk-Kul</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> The first three World Nomad Games were held in <a href="/wiki/Cholpon-Ata" title="Cholpon-Ata">Cholpon-Ata</a>, Kyrgyzstan. The 6th International Sports Festival Pearl of Kyrgyzstan were held in Issyk-Kul region from June 15 to July 3 of 2022.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Horse_riding">Horse riding</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Horse riding">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The traditional national sports reflect the importance of <a href="/wiki/Horse_riding" class="mw-redirect" title="Horse riding">horse riding</a> in Kyrgyz culture.
</p><p>Very popular, as in all of Central Asia, is <i><a href="/wiki/Buzkashi" title="Buzkashi">Ulak Tartysh</a></i>, a <a href="/wiki/Team_sports" class="mw-redirect" title="Team sports">team game</a> resembling a cross between <a href="/wiki/Polo" title="Polo">polo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rugby_football" title="Rugby football">rugby</a> in which two teams of riders wrestle for possession of the headless carcass of a goat, which they attempt to deliver across the opposition's goal line, or into the opposition's goal: a big tub or a circle marked on the ground.
</p><p>Other popular games on horseback include:
</p>
<ul><li><i>At Chabysh</i> – a long-distance horse race, sometimes over a distance of more than 50&#160;km</li>
<li><i>Jumby Atmai</i> – a large bar of precious metal (the "jumby") is tied to a pole by a thread and contestants attempt to break the thread by shooting at it, while at a gallop</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Kyz_Kuumai" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyz Kuumai">Kyz Kuumai</a></i> – a man chases a girl in order to win a kiss from her, while she gallops away; if he is not successful she may in turn chase him and attempt to beat him with her "kamchi" (horsewhip)</li>
<li><i>Oodarysh</i> – two contestants wrestle on horseback, each attempting to be the first to throw the other from his horse</li>
<li><i>Tyin Emmei</i> – picking up a coin from the ground at full gallop</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Education in Kyrgyzstan">Education in Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<p>The school system in Kyrgyzstan includes primary (grades 1 to 4, some schools have optional 0 grade), secondary (grades 5 to 9) and high (grades 10 to 11) divisions within one school.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Children are usually accepted to primary schools at the age of 6 or 7. It is required that every child finishes 9 grades of school and receives a certificate of completion. Grades 10–11 are optional, but it is necessary to complete them to graduate and receive a state-accredited school diploma. To graduate, a student must complete the 11-year school course and pass 4 mandatory state exams in writing, maths, history and a foreign language.
</p><p>There are 77 public schools in Bishkek (capital city) and more than 200 in the rest of the country. There are 55 higher educational institutions and universities in Kyrgyzstan, out of which 37 are state institutions.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In September 2016, the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Central_Asia" title="University of Central Asia">University of Central Asia</a> was launched in Naryn, Kyrgyzstan.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are also various Russian-language medium schools in Bishkek, Osh and other areas. Because of the better funding that they receive in comparation with Kyrgyz state schools, many Kyrgyz go there. In March 2021 Russia announced its plans to create approximately 30 new Russian-language schools in Kyrgyzstan. Teachers from Russia are also working here. However, the existence of these schools has been criticised, for reasons such as the fact that Russian language education has flaws compared to the Turkish and American schools in the country, but also because many ethnic Kyrgyz born after Kyrgyz independence in 1991 can't speak Kyrgyz, but only Russian, according to a Bishkek resident.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Libraries">Libraries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Libraries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kyrgyzstan is home to 1,066 libraries.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/National_Library_of_the_Kyrgyz_Republic" title="National Library of the Kyrgyz Republic">National Library of the Kyrgyz Republic</a> is the oldest library in the country, which was established in 1934. Kyrgyz Libraries are working towards expanding access to communities, evident in projects such as the signing of the <a href="/wiki/Marrakesh_VIP_Treaty" title="Marrakesh VIP Treaty">Marrakesh VIP Treaty</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Open_access" title="Open access">Open access</a> Portal.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg/220px-E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg/330px-E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg/440px-E8102-Bishkek-West-Bus-Terminal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a><figcaption>Bishkek West Bus Terminal</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Kyrgyzstan" title="Transport in Kyrgyzstan">Transport in Kyrgyzstan</a></div>
<p>Transport in Kyrgyzstan is severely constrained by the country's alpine topography. Roads have to snake up steep valleys, cross passes of 3,000 metres (9,800&#160;ft) altitude and more, and are subject to frequent mudslides and snow avalanches. Winter travel is close to impossible in many of the more remote and high-altitude regions.
</p><p>Additional problems come from the fact that many roads and railway lines built during the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet</a> period are today intersected by international boundaries, requiring time-consuming border formalities to cross where they are not completely closed. <a href="/wiki/Horse" title="Horse">Horses</a> are still a much-used transport option, especially in more rural areas; Kyrgyzstan's road infrastructure is not extensive, so horses are able to reach locations that motor vehicles cannot, and they do not require expensive, imported <a href="/wiki/Fuel" title="Fuel">fuel</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Airports">Airports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Airports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>At the end of the Soviet period there were about 50 airports and airstrips in Kyrgyzstan, many of them built primarily to serve military purposes in this border region so close to China. Only a few of them remain in service today. The <a href="/wiki/Kyrgyzstan_Air_Company" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyrgyzstan Air Company">Kyrgyzstan Air Company</a> provides air transport to China, Russia, and other local countries.
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Manas_International_Airport" title="Manas International Airport">Manas International Airport</a> near <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a> is the main international airport, with services to Moscow, <a href="/wiki/Tashkent" title="Tashkent">Tashkent</a>, <a href="/wiki/Almaty" title="Almaty">Almaty</a>, <a href="/wiki/Urumqi" class="mw-redirect" title="Urumqi">Urumqi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Istanbul" title="Istanbul">Istanbul</a>, <a href="/wiki/Baku" title="Baku">Baku</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dubai" title="Dubai">Dubai</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Osh_Airport" title="Osh Airport">Osh Airport</a> is the main air terminal in the south of the country, with daily connections to Bishkek, and services to Moscow, Krasnoyarsk, Almaty and more international places.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jalal-Abad_Airport" title="Jalal-Abad Airport">Jalal-Abad Airport</a> is linked to Bishkek by daily flights. The national flag carrier, Kyrgyzstan, operates flights on <a href="/wiki/BAe-146" class="mw-redirect" title="BAe-146">BAe-146</a> aircraft. During the summer months, a weekly flight links Jalal-Abad with the Issyk-Kul Region.</li>
<li>Other facilities built during the Soviet era are either closed down, used only occasionally or restricted to military use (e.g., <a href="/wiki/Kant_Air_Base" class="mw-redirect" title="Kant Air Base">Kant Air Base</a> near Bishkek, which is used by the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Air_Force" title="Russian Air Force">Russian Air Force</a>).</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Banned_airline_status">Banned airline status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Banned airline status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kyrgyzstan appears on the European Union's <a href="/wiki/List_of_air_carriers_banned_in_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="List of air carriers banned in the European Union">list of prohibited countries for the certification of airlines</a>. This means that no airline that is registered in Kyrgyzstan may operate services of any kind within the European Union, due to safety standards that fail to meet European regulations.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Railways">Railways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Railways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Ch%C3%BCy_Valley" title="Chüy Valley">Chüy Valley</a> in the north and the <a href="/wiki/Fergana_valley" class="mw-redirect" title="Fergana valley">Fergana valley</a> in the south were endpoints of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>'s rail system in Central Asia. Following the emergence of independent post-Soviet states, the rail lines which were built without regard for administrative boundaries have been cut by borders, and traffic is therefore severely curtailed. The small bits of rail lines within Kyrgyzstan, about 370&#160;km (230&#160;mi) (1,520&#160;mm (59.8&#160;in) broad gauge) in total, have little economic value in the absence of the former bulk traffic over long distances to and from such centres as <a href="/wiki/Tashkent" title="Tashkent">Tashkent</a>, <a href="/wiki/Almaty" title="Almaty">Almaty</a>, and the cities of Russia.
</p><p>In 2022, construction began on a new 186&#160;km extension of the existing railway from <a href="/wiki/Balykchy" title="Balykchy">Balykchy</a> to <a href="/wiki/Karakeche" title="Karakeche">Karakeche</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> Its primary purpose will be to carry coal from mines at Karakeche to <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a>.
</p><p>There are vague plans about extending rail lines from <a href="/wiki/Balykchy" title="Balykchy">Balykchy</a> in the north or from <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a> in the south into China, but the cost of construction would be enormous.
</p><p>In 2022, the president of Kyrgyzstan, Zhaparov, has told local media that he expects construction of the 523&#160;km China–Kyrgyzstan–Uzbekistan (CKU) Railway to begin next year. The CKU Railway would comprise 213&#160;km (132&#160;mi) in China, 260&#160;km (162&#160;mi) in Kyrgyzstan and 50&#160;km (31&#160;mi) in Uzbekistan.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Rail_connections_with_adjacent_countries">Rail connections with adjacent countries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Rail connections with adjacent countries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Highways">Highways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Highways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg/220px-Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg/330px-Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg/440px-Osh_03-2016_img07_Gapar_Aitiev_Street.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5023" data-file-height="3139" /></a><figcaption>Street scene in <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>With support from the <a href="/wiki/Asian_Development_Bank" title="Asian Development Bank">Asian Development Bank</a>, a major road linking the north and southwest from <a href="/wiki/Bishkek" title="Bishkek">Bishkek</a> to <a href="/wiki/Osh" title="Osh">Osh</a> has recently been completed. This considerably eases communication between the two major population centres of the country—the <a href="/wiki/Ch%C3%BCy_Valley" title="Chüy Valley">Chüy Valley</a> in the north and the <a href="/wiki/Fergana_Valley" title="Fergana Valley">Fergana Valley</a> in the South. An offshoot of this road branches off across a 3,500 meter <a href="/wiki/Mountain_pass" title="Mountain pass">pass</a> into the <a href="/wiki/Talas_Valley" class="mw-redirect" title="Talas Valley">Talas Valley</a> in the northwest. Plans are now being formulated to build a major road from Osh into China.
</p>
<ul><li><i>total:</i> 34,000&#160;km (21,127&#160;mi) (including 140&#160;km (87&#160;mi) of expressways)</li>
<li><i>paved:</i> 22,600&#160;km (14,043&#160;mi) (includes some all-weather gravel-surfaced roads)</li>
<li><i>unpaved:</i> 7,700&#160;km (4,785&#160;mi) (these roads are made of unstabilized earth and are difficult to negotiate in wet weather) (1990)</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ports_and_harbours">Ports and harbours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Kyrgyzstan&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Ports and harbours">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Balykchy" title="Balykchy">Balykchy</a> (Ysyk-Kol or Rybach'ye) on Issyk Kul Lake.</li></ul>
